No… not in line with who I thought I was. I keep saying that because I don’t know how else to explain it. I didn’t think of myself as someone who would be cruel or uncaring. I tried to be decent. I tried to be faithful. But then I look at what happened, and I think, well, maybe this is who I really was underneath.

I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back. And it’s not just that one thing, Doctor, it’s that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I’m ashamed of, thoughts that make me feel like there was something rotten in me. So when I put it all together, I can’t just say, “That wasn’t me.” It feels like it came from me, and that’s what scares me.

And I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know people don’t like when I say that, and they tell me not to think that way, but I know it. It feels plain to me. Like my sin, my coldness, whatever was in my heart, has brought trouble down on them. So no, it’s not the personality I wanted to have, but I’m afraid it shows the truth about me.

I wish I could say a prayer and then feel clean of it, or feel like it was settled, but it doesn’t work that way. I’ll pray, and then I’m right back where I started, thinking about what I should’ve done and what was in my heart. So… no, I don’t have a certain repeated thing that makes the thoughts go away. Maybe other people have bad thoughts and just let them pass, I don’t know. For me it feels like I have to examine them, like I have to know whether they show something about me. So no, I’m not sure I’d say they’re forced upon me. I’d say they feel wrong, and I wish they weren’t there, but I’m afraid they are something I would think, and that’s what frightens me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, yes and no. They’re not in line with what I was raised to believe, or what I want to believe. I don’t want to be ugly-hearted. I don’t want to be selfish or cold. I’ve tried to be a good Christian woman, I’ve tried to do right by people. So in that way, no, those thoughts don’t feel like what my values are supposed to be.\n\nBut then I keep thinking… if they were in me, if I had them, then maybe that means my values weren’t real enough. Maybe the Lord saw what was really underneath. That’s what I can’t get away from. I keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died, because she reached out and I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I feel like those things are tied together. Like it wasn’t just a missed phone call, it was proof that something was wrong in me.\n\nAnd now I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. I know it sounds terrible to say out loud, but I can’t see it any other way. The Lord knows what was in my heart, even if I tried to act decent on the outside. So no, they don’t match what I want my beliefs to be… but I’m afraid they show what I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something you would normally think and agree with, or are they disturbing to you and go against what you believe in?\n\nPatient: They’re disturbing to me, Doctor. I don’t want to agree with them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who has ugly thoughts in her heart. But the trouble is, I keep thinking that because I had them at all, they must mean something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it before, but I keep telling them—I keep telling people—I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out, and I didn’t call her back. And then I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, and it all feels tied together, like it shows I was selfish, or cold, or not the kind of Christian woman I thought I was.\n\nSo no, they don’t go along with what I believe in, not what I want to believe in. But I can’t just say, “Oh, those weren’t me,” either. The Lord saw all of it. He saw I didn’t answer her, and He saw what was in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. That’s what I can’t get away from. It feels like judgment. Like proof.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would normally think and are in line with your own personality?\n\nPatient: No… not in line with who I thought I was. I keep saying that because I don’t know how else to explain it. I didn’t think of myself as someone who would be cruel or uncaring. I tried to be decent. I tried to be faithful. But then I look at what happened, and I think, well, maybe this is who I really was underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back. And it’s not just that one thing, Doctor, it’s that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I’m ashamed of, thoughts that make me feel like there was something rotten in me. So when I put it all together, I can’t just say, “That wasn’t me.” It feels like it came from me, and that’s what scares me.\n\nAnd I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know people don’t like when I say that, and they tell me not to think that way, but I know it. It feels plain to me. Like my sin, my coldness, whatever was in my heart, has brought trouble down on them. So no, it’s not the personality I wanted to have, but I’m afraid it shows the truth about me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

But I can’t just say, “Oh, those weren’t me,” either. The Lord saw all of it. He saw I didn’t answer her, and He saw what was in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. That’s what I can’t get away from. It feels like judgment. Like proof.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would normally think and are in line with your own personality?\n\nPatient: No… not in line with who I thought I was. But then I look at what happened, and I think, well, maybe this is who I really was underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. Like my sin, my coldness, whatever was in my heart, has brought trouble down on them. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I… I don’t want them to be part of who I am. That’s the honest answer. I don’t want to think that way, and I don’t feel proud of it, not at all. But I keep coming back to the same thing, Doctor—if those ugly thoughts were in me, even for a moment, then maybe they do show something about me.

I know I’ve said it already, and I’m sorry, but I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me and I didn’t call her back. I keep seeing that over and over in my mind, like there was this chance to do the right thing and I failed her. And then when I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, it feels like it all fits together, like maybe I was cold or selfish and didn’t even see it clearly at the time.

So no, I wouldn’t have said those thoughts were normally me. I would’ve said I was trying to be a decent woman. But now… now I can’t separate them from me. I feel like the Lord saw what was really in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. I know that sounds awful, but it feels true. It feels like my fault has spread onto them somehow. So I don’t want it to be who I am, but I’m afraid it is.

I know what’s going on, though. It’s me. I’ve been trying to tell them that I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I should have, and I can’t stop going over it in my head. If I had picked up the phone, maybe she’d still be here.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts I shouldn’t have had, and I feel like the Lord sees all of that. He knows what was in my heart. I keep telling them everybody’s suffering now because of me, even the church, like He’s punishing them because I did wrong. They tell me not to say that, but I know it. I keep thinking if I had just called her back, if I hadn’t waited, if I hadn’t been so wrapped up in myself, maybe she wouldn’t have died. People tell me it isn’t my fault, but I don’t believe that. It doesn’t go away just because I want it to.\n\nIt’s more like I sit there and go over it again and again, trying to understand how I could’ve let it happen, or what I should’ve done differently. People might say, “Just stop thinking about it,” but I don’t know how you’re supposed to do that when it feels true. I don’t have some ritual or anything, if that’s what you mean. I just… I keep thinking about it, and I keep feeling like I need to be forgiven, but I don’t know what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain actions or prayers to try and make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, no. I mean, I do pray, because I don’t know what else to do when my mind gets like that, but it isn’t like I have to say the same words a certain number of times, or touch something, or do something over and over to make it stop.\n\nI wish there was something like that, honestly. I wish I could say a prayer and then feel clean of it, or feel like it was settled, but it doesn’t work that way. I’ll pray, and then I’m right back where I started, thinking about what I should’ve done and what was in my heart. So… no, I don’t have a certain repeated thing that makes the thoughts go away. They just keep coming anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your daily activities because of these thoughts and feelings of guilt?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I am. More than I want to admit, I think.\n\nI’ve missed shifts at the store, and I don’t like saying that because I’ve always been someone who shows up. Even when I was tired or didn’t feel good, I’d go in and stand at that register. But lately I just… I can’t get myself together some mornings, or I get there and my mind isn’t where it’s supposed to be. I have mail sitting there, bills I haven’t even opened because I look at the envelopes and I just feel this heaviness come over me. I tell myself I’ll do it later, and then later comes and I still don’t. That used to be how I kept connected, you know, seeing the ladies, checking in on folks, doing what I could. It’s more this heavy feeling that I owe something and I don’t know how to pay it. Like no matter what I do, it wouldn’t really be enough.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have a certain action I have to perform. I just keep feeling like I need to make it right, and I can’t figure out how.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming need to confess your thoughts or actions to someone, like a priest or a family member, to feel relieved?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I don’t know if it makes me relieved exactly, but I keep trying. And it’s not just that. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts I shouldn’t have had, and I feel like the Lord saw all of that.\n\nSo I keep saying it, like I need someone to understand that it was me. They don’t know how dark it was sometimes.\n\nAnd I’ve told them I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know that sounds awful, but that’s how it feels. Like my sin, my failing, has spread out and other people are suffering for it. So yes, I do feel like I have to confess it, again and again, to my niece, to people at church, anybody who will listen. But it doesn’t really wash it away. It only quiets it for a minute, if that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to prevent anything bad from happening to others because of what you think you did wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do, in a way. Not like there’s one exact thing I can do that would fix it, but I feel like I have to keep warning people, or telling them, because if they don’t understand what I’ve done, then maybe more bad will come from it.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I should have, and I keep thinking if I had just picked up the phone, if I had been less selfish, less full of those ugly thoughts I had sometimes… maybe she’d still be here. And then it feels like it didn’t stop with her. It feels like the Lord saw all of it, every part of my heart, and now everybody at church is being punished because of me.\n\nSo I don’t know what I’m supposed to do to stop it. I pray, I tell people, I try to stay away sometimes because I don’t want to bring that punishment near them. But I don’t know if that helps. I just feel like if I don’t do something, if I don’t keep confessing it or trying to make them see, then something else terrible could happen because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to undo the harm you think you've caused?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that. I don’t know how to undo it, that’s the terrible part. If there were some clear thing I could do, I think I would do it, but I don’t know what would ever be enough.\n\nI keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died. I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, because of what I failed to do and because of what was in my heart. I feel like He saw that.\n\nSo yes, I guess I’m hoping to be forgiven, but I don’t really feel like I deserve it. And I’m scared of the punishment too, not just for me. I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, like my sin spilled over onto them somehow. I’m not confessing just to feel better. I’m confessing because it feels true, and because if I don’t say it, then it’s like I’m hiding it from God and from everyone. And I’m afraid that would make it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t know if I’d say they come in without me wanting them to, exactly. That’s part of what scares me, because I keep wondering what it says about me that they were there at all.\n\nSometimes it feels like I’m just trying to pray or sit quietly, and then I remember something awful I thought or felt, or I worry that maybe I meant it more than I want to admit. But I don’t know if that’s the same as them “coming into my mind.” It’s more like I keep turning them over and over, trying to figure out if they were really mine, if they were sinful, if they caused something.\n\nI don’t want to be the kind of person who has thoughts like that. I can say that. I don’t want them. But then I think… if they came from me, then maybe wanting them gone now doesn’t excuse that they were there. They’re not in line with what I was raised to believe, or what I want to believe. I don’t want to be ugly-hearted. I don’t want to be selfish or cold. I’ve tried to be a good Christian woman, I’ve tried to do right by people. So in that way, no, those thoughts don’t feel like what my values are supposed to be.\n\nBut then I keep thinking… if they were in me, if I had them, then maybe that means my values weren’t real enough. Maybe the Lord saw what was really underneath. That’s what I can’t get away from. I keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died, because she reached out and I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I feel like those things are tied together. Like it wasn’t just a missed phone call, it was proof that something was wrong in me.\n\nAnd now I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. I know it sounds terrible to say out loud, but I can’t see it any other way. The Lord knows what was in my heart, even if I tried to act decent on the outside. So no, they don’t match what I want my beliefs to be… but I’m afraid they show what I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something you would normally think and agree with, or are they disturbing to you and go against what you believe in?\n\nPatient: They’re disturbing to me, Doctor. I don’t want to agree with them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who has ugly thoughts in her heart. But the trouble is, I keep thinking that because I had them at all, they must mean something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it before, but I keep telling them—I keep telling people—I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out, and I didn’t call her back. And then I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, and it all feels tied together, like it shows I was selfish, or cold, or not the kind of Christian woman I thought I was.\n\nSo no, they don’t go along with what I believe in, not what I want to believe in. But I can’t just say, “Oh, those weren’t me,” either. The Lord saw all of it. He saw I didn’t answer her, and He saw what was in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. That’s what I can’t get away from. It feels like judgment. But then I look at what happened, and I think, well, maybe this is who I really was underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back. And it’s not just that one thing, Doctor, it’s that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I’m ashamed of, thoughts that make me feel like there was something rotten in me. So when I put it all together, I can’t just say, “That wasn’t me.” It feels like it came from me, and that’s what scares me.\n\nAnd I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know people don’t like when I say that, and they tell me not to think that way, but I know it. It feels plain to me. Like my sin, my coldness, whatever was in my heart, has brought trouble down on them. So no, it’s not the personality I wanted to have, but I’m afraid it shows the truth about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally think and are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want them to be part of who I am. That’s the honest answer. I don’t want to think that way, and I don’t feel proud of it, not at all. But I keep coming back to the same thing, Doctor—if those ugly thoughts were in me, even for a moment, then maybe they do show something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it already, and I’m sorry, but I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I came because… well, my family keeps saying I need to talk to somebody, and the people at church are worried too, I think. I know what’s going on, though. It’s me. I’ve been trying to tell them that I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I should have, and I can’t stop going over it in my head. If I had picked up the phone, maybe she’d still be here.\n\nAnd it’s not just that. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts I shouldn’t have had, and I feel like the Lord sees all of that. I just… I keep thinking about it, and I keep feeling like I need to be forgiven, but I don’t know what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain actions or prayers to try and make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, no. I mean, I do pray, because I don’t know what else to do when my mind gets like that, but it isn’t like I have to say the same words a certain number of times, or touch something, or do something over and over to make it stop.\n\nI wish there was something like that, honestly. I wish I could say a prayer and then feel clean of it, or feel like it was settled, but it doesn’t work that way. I’ll pray, and then I’m right back where I started, thinking about what I should’ve done and what was in my heart. So… no, I don’t have a certain repeated thing that makes the thoughts go away. They just keep coming anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your daily activities because of these thoughts and feelings of guilt?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I am. It’s more this heavy feeling that I owe something and I don’t know how to pay it. Like no matter what I do, it wouldn’t really be enough.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have a certain action I have to perform. I just keep feeling like I need to make it right, and I can’t figure out how.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming need to confess your thoughts or actions to someone, like a priest or a family member, to feel relieved?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I don’t know if it makes me relieved exactly, but I keep trying. I don’t know how to undo it, that’s the terrible part. That’s part of what scares me, because I keep wondering what it says about me that they were there at all.\n\nSometimes it feels like I’m just trying to pray or sit quietly, and then I remember something awful I thought or felt, or I worry that maybe I meant it more than I want to admit. I can say that. I don’t want them. But then I think… if they came from me, then maybe wanting them gone now doesn’t excuse that they were there. I wish I could say they were alien, like they were just some strange thing passing through me that didn’t belong to me. That would be a comfort, I suppose. So no, I’m not sure I’d say they’re forced upon me. I’d say they feel wrong, and I wish they weren’t there, but I’m afraid they are something I would think, and that’s what frightens me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, yes and no. They’re not in line with what I was raised to believe, or what I want to believe. I don’t want to be ugly-hearted. I don’t want to be selfish or cold. I’ve tried to be a good Christian woman, I’ve tried to do right by people. So in that way, no, those thoughts don’t feel like what my values are supposed to be.\n\nBut then I keep thinking… if they were in me, if I had them, then maybe that means my values weren’t real enough. I keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died, because she reached out and I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I feel like those things are tied together. I don’t want to agree with them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who has ugly thoughts in her heart. She reached out, and I didn’t call her back. And then I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, and it all feels tied together, like it shows I was selfish, or cold, or not the kind of Christian woman I thought I was.\n\nSo no, they don’t go along with what I believe in, not what I want to believe in. But I can’t just say, “Oh, those weren’t me,” either. The Lord saw all of it. He saw I didn’t answer her, and He saw what was in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. That’s what I can’t get away from. It feels like judgment. Like proof.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would normally think and are in line with your own personality?\n\nPatient: No… not in line with who I thought I was. Like proof.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would normally think and are in line with your own personality?\n\nPatient: No… not in line with who I thought I was. But then I look at what happened, and I think, well, maybe this is who I really was underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back. Like my sin, my coldness, whatever was in my heart, has brought trouble down on them. So no, it’s not the personality I wanted to have, but I’m afraid it shows the truth about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally think and are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want them to be part of who I am. But I keep coming back to the same thing, Doctor—if those ugly thoughts were in me, even for a moment, then maybe they do show something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it already, and I’m sorry, but I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
They don’t feel like a voice from outside, if that’s what you mean. They feel like they’re in me, and that’s the worst part. I wish I could say they were just something strange that came from somewhere else, something I could push away and say, “That isn’t mine.” But I can’t do that.

I keep going back to my friend. She reached out, and I didn’t call her back. I keep telling people that I’m the reason she died, because I didn’t do what I should have done. And then when I remember that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I’m ashamed of, it feels like it all came from the same place inside me. Like it shows there was something wrong with my heart.

I know people keep telling me not to say that, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. That doesn’t feel like it’s coming from outside myself either. It feels like something I know, like a truth I can’t get away from. So… no, not outside. It feels like it’s me. And I hate that.

It doesn’t go away just because I want it to.\n\nIt’s more like I sit there and go over it again and again, trying to understand how I could’ve let it happen, or what I should’ve done differently. I just… I keep thinking about it, and I keep feeling like I need to be forgiven, but I don’t know what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain actions or prayers to try and make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, no. I mean, I do pray, because I don’t know what else to do when my mind gets like that, but it isn’t like I have to say the same words a certain number of times, or touch something, or do something over and over to make it stop.\n\nI wish there was something like that, honestly. I wish I could say a prayer and then feel clean of it, or feel like it was settled, but it doesn’t work that way. I’ll pray, and then I’m right back where I started, thinking about what I should’ve done and what was in my heart. So… no, I don’t have a certain repeated thing that makes the thoughts go away. They just keep coming anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your daily activities because of these thoughts and feelings of guilt?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I am. More than I want to admit, I think.\n\nI’ve missed shifts at the store, and I don’t like saying that because I’ve always been someone who shows up. Even when I was tired or didn’t feel good, I’d go in and stand at that register. But lately I just… I can’t get myself together some mornings, or I get there and my mind isn’t where it’s supposed to be. I’ve made mistakes at the register, not little ones like forgetting a bag, but enough that my supervisor started looking at me different and asking if I was all right. My niece got worried too, because she knows I don’t usually let work slip like that.\n\nAt home it’s not much better. I have mail sitting there, bills I haven’t even opened because I look at the envelopes and I just feel this heaviness come over me. I tell myself I’ll do it later, and then later comes and I still don’t. It piles up, and then I feel worse because that’s another thing I’m failing at.\n\nAnd church… I haven’t been going to the groups or visiting people the way I used to. That used to be how I kept connected, you know, seeing the ladies, checking in on folks, doing what I could. But now I don’t feel right being there, or I feel like I bring something bad with me, so I stay away. Then I’m alone more, and the thoughts just get louder. So yes, it’s gotten in the way of things. A lot.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you must perform any specific actions to make up for what you think you did wrong?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I don’t think there’s anything specific, no. It’s not like I have a clear thing in my head, like “if I do this one action, then it balances out,” or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I think maybe I ought to be kinder, or give more, or pray more, or just keep myself out of people’s way so I don’t cause any more harm… but it’s not a rule, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I owe something and I don’t know how to pay it. Like no matter what I do, it wouldn’t really be enough.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have a certain action I have to perform. I just keep feeling like I need to make it right, and I can’t figure out how.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming need to confess your thoughts or actions to someone, like a priest or a family member, to feel relieved?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I don’t know if it makes me relieved exactly, but I keep trying. I keep telling people, because I feel like they need to know what I did, or what I failed to do.\n\nI’ve told my family, and I’ve said it at church too, that I’m the reason my friend died. She called me, and I didn’t call her back, and I keep going over that in my head. If I had called… if I had been a better friend, a better Christian, maybe things would’ve been different. And it’s not just that. I had ugly thoughts sometimes, thoughts I shouldn’t have had, and I feel like the Lord saw all of that.\n\nSo I keep saying it, like I need someone to understand that it was me. They don’t know how dark it was sometimes.\n\nAnd I’ve told them I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. I know that sounds awful, but that’s how it feels. Like my sin, my failing, has spread out and other people are suffering for it. So yes, I do feel like I have to confess it, again and again, to my niece, to people at church, anybody who will listen. But it doesn’t really wash it away. It only quiets it for a minute, if that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to prevent anything bad from happening to others because of what you think you did wrong?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I do, in a way. Not like there’s one exact thing I can do that would fix it, but I feel like I have to keep warning people, or telling them, because if they don’t understand what I’ve done, then maybe more bad will come from it.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. I didn’t call her back when I should have, and I keep thinking if I had just picked up the phone, if I had been less selfish, less full of those ugly thoughts I had sometimes… maybe she’d still be here. And then it feels like it didn’t stop with her. It feels like the Lord saw all of it, every part of my heart, and now everybody at church is being punished because of me.\n\nSo I don’t know what I’m supposed to do to stop it. I pray, I tell people, I try to stay away sometimes because I don’t want to bring that punishment near them. But I don’t know if that helps. I just feel like if I don’t do something, if I don’t keep confessing it or trying to make them see, then something else terrible could happen because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to undo the harm you think you've caused?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that. I don’t know how to undo it, that’s the terrible part. If there were some clear thing I could do, I think I would do it, but I don’t know what would ever be enough.\n\nI keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died. I know everyone keeps saying I didn’t know, that I can’t put that on myself, but I didn’t call her back. She reached out, and I didn’t answer the way I should have. I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, because of what I failed to do and because of what was in my heart. It just sits there.\n\nDoctor: When you confess your thoughts and actions to others, are you hoping to avoid punishment or to feel forgiven?\n\nPatient: I think… both, maybe. I don’t know how to separate them anymore.\n\nWhen I tell people, when I say it out loud again, that I’m the reason my friend died, part of me is hoping somebody will say something that makes it not true. And it wasn’t just the phone call, it was what was in me too, those ugly thoughts I had sometimes, the selfishness, the hardness in my heart. I feel like He saw that.\n\nSo yes, I guess I’m hoping to be forgiven, but I don’t really feel like I deserve it. And I’m scared of the punishment too, not just for me. I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, like my sin spilled over onto them somehow. So when I confess, I’m trying to make it known, trying to say, “It was me, I did this, don’t punish them.” But it doesn’t feel like enough.\n\nPeople tell me I’m not responsible, but they don’t understand. I’m not confessing just to feel better. I’m confessing because it feels true, and because if I don’t say it, then it’s like I’m hiding it from God and from everyone. And I’m afraid that would make it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t know if I’d say they come in without me wanting them to, exactly. That’s part of what scares me, because I keep wondering what it says about me that they were there at all.\n\nSometimes it feels like I’m just trying to pray or sit quietly, and then I remember something awful I thought or felt, or I worry that maybe I meant it more than I want to admit. But I don’t know if that’s the same as them “coming into my mind.” It’s more like I keep turning them over and over, trying to figure out if they were really mine, if they were sinful, if they caused something.\n\nI don’t want to be the kind of person who has thoughts like that. I can say that. I don’t want them. But then I think… if they came from me, then maybe wanting them gone now doesn’t excuse that they were there. I’m sorry, I don’t know if I’m answering the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are a part of who you are, or do they feel alien to you?\n\nPatient: They feel like… I don’t know. I wish I could say they were alien, like they were just some strange thing passing through me that didn’t belong to me. That would be a comfort, I suppose. But I don’t feel that way, not really.\n\nI keep thinking they must be part of me, because they came from inside me. Those ugly thoughts, the selfish ones, the hard ones… even if I didn’t want to think of myself that way, they were there. And then my friend reached out and I didn’t call her back, and now I keep telling people I’m the reason she died, because it all feels connected. Like the thoughts showed what was really in my heart, and then I failed her when it mattered.\n\nPeople try to tell me, “That isn’t who you are,” but I don’t know how they can know that. And I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, because of what was in me and because I didn’t answer her. So no… I don’t think they feel alien. They feel like evidence. Like proof of what I really am, even if I hate saying that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are forced upon you and are not something you would normally think?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “forced upon me” is the right way to say it. That sounds like they’re coming from somewhere else, or like I’m being made to think them, and I don’t… I don’t really know that I can say that.\n\nThey don’t feel normal, exactly. I don’t like them. I don’t sit there wanting to have ugly thoughts or wanting to feel hard-hearted. But when they come up, or when I remember them, it feels like they’re mine somehow. Like they came out of me, and that’s what makes it so upsetting. If they were just forced on me, maybe I could push them away and say, “That’s not me.” But I can’t seem to do that.\n\nI keep going back and forth in my head, trying to decide what it means that I had them at all. So no, I’m not sure I’d say they’re forced upon me. I’d say they feel wrong, and I wish they weren’t there, but I’m afraid they are something I would think, and that’s what frightens me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, yes and no. They’re not in line with what I was raised to believe, or what I want to believe. I don’t want to be ugly-hearted. I don’t want to be selfish or cold. I’ve tried to be a good Christian woman, I’ve tried to do right by people. So in that way, no, those thoughts don’t feel like what my values are supposed to be.\n\nBut then I keep thinking… if they were in me, if I had them, then maybe that means my values weren’t real enough. Maybe the Lord saw what was really underneath. That’s what I can’t get away from. I keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died, because she reached out and I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I feel like those things are tied together. Like it wasn’t just a missed phone call, it was proof that something was wrong in me.\n\nAnd now I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. I know it sounds terrible to say out loud, but I can’t see it any other way. The Lord knows what was in my heart, even if I tried to act decent on the outside. So no, they don’t match what I want my beliefs to be… but I’m afraid they show what I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something you would normally think and agree with, or are they disturbing to you and go against what you believe in?\n\nPatient: They’re disturbing to me, Doctor. I don’t want to agree with them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who has ugly thoughts in her heart. But the trouble is, I keep thinking that because I had them at all, they must mean something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it before, but I keep telling them—I keep telling people—I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out, and I didn’t call her back. And then I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, and it all feels tied together, like it shows I was selfish, or cold, or not the kind of Christian woman I thought I was.\n\nSo no, they don’t go along with what I believe in, not what I want to believe in. But I can’t just say, “Oh, those weren’t me,” either. The Lord saw all of it. He saw I didn’t answer her, and He saw what was in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. That’s what I can’t get away from. It feels like judgment. Like proof.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would normally think and are in line with your own personality?\n\nPatient: No… not in line with who I thought I was. I keep saying that because I don’t know how else to explain it. I didn’t think of myself as someone who would be cruel or uncaring. I tried to be decent. I tried to be faithful. But then I look at what happened, and I think, well, maybe this is who I really was underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back. And it’s not just that one thing, Doctor, it’s that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I’m ashamed of, thoughts that make me feel like there was something rotten in me. So when I put it all together, I can’t just say, “That wasn’t me.” It feels like it came from me, and that’s what scares me.\n\nAnd I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. Like my sin, my coldness, whatever was in my heart, has brought trouble down on them. So no, it’s not the personality I wanted to have, but I’m afraid it shows the truth about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally think and are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want them to be part of who I am. That’s the honest answer. I don’t want to think that way, and I don’t feel proud of it, not at all. But I keep coming back to the same thing, Doctor—if those ugly thoughts were in me, even for a moment, then maybe they do show something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it already, and I’m sorry, but I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me and I didn’t call her back. I keep seeing that over and over in my mind, like there was this chance to do the right thing and I failed her. And then when I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, it feels like it all fits together, like maybe I was cold or selfish and didn’t even see it clearly at the time.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t have said those thoughts were normally me. I would’ve said I was trying to be a decent woman. But now… now I can’t separate them from me. I feel like the Lord saw what was really in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. I know that sounds awful, but it feels true. It feels like my fault has spread onto them somehow. So I don’t want it to be who I am, but I’m afraid it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally think and are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they are coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: They don’t feel like a voice from outside, if that’s what you mean. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… I mean, they’re not consistent with what I wanted my values to be. I always thought I tried to be a decent person, tried to do right by people, tried to be faithful. But now I keep thinking maybe that was just what I wanted to believe about myself.

Because if I really had good values, why didn’t I call my friend back? She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling them I’m the reason she died. I can’t get away from that. It feels like proof that there was something selfish or cold in me. And then I remember that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I shouldn’t have had, thoughts I’m ashamed were even in my head, and it makes me feel like maybe those thoughts were closer to the truth of me than all the good things I tried to be.

And I know people say I’m taking too much onto myself, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. That’s what makes it feel like it is about who I am, not just a passing thought. It feels like something wrong in me has caused harm beyond just me. So… no, they don’t match the person I wanted to be. But I’m afraid they show who I really am.

It doesn’t go away just because I want it to.\n\nIt’s more like I sit there and go over it again and again, trying to understand how I could’ve let it happen, or what I should’ve done differently. I just… I keep thinking about it, and I keep feeling like I need to be forgiven, but I don’t know what would be enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to repeat certain actions or prayers to try and make these ugly thoughts go away?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like that, no. I mean, I do pray, because I don’t know what else to do when my mind gets like that, but it isn’t like I have to say the same words a certain number of times, or touch something, or do something over and over to make it stop.\n\nI wish there was something like that, honestly. I wish I could say a prayer and then feel clean of it, or feel like it was settled, but it doesn’t work that way. I’ll pray, and then I’m right back where I started, thinking about what I should’ve done and what was in my heart. So… no, I don’t have a certain repeated thing that makes the thoughts go away. They just keep coming anyway.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble doing your daily activities because of these thoughts and feelings of guilt?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I am. More than I want to admit, I think.\n\nI’ve missed shifts at the store, and I don’t like saying that because I’ve always been someone who shows up. Even when I was tired or didn’t feel good, I’d go in and stand at that register. I have mail sitting there, bills I haven’t even opened because I look at the envelopes and I just feel this heaviness come over me. It’s not like I have a clear thing in my head, like “if I do this one action, then it balances out,” or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I think maybe I ought to be kinder, or give more, or pray more, or just keep myself out of people’s way so I don’t cause any more harm… but it’s not a rule, exactly. It’s more this heavy feeling that I owe something and I don’t know how to pay it. Like no matter what I do, it wouldn’t really be enough.\n\nSo… no, I don’t have a certain action I have to perform. I just keep feeling like I need to make it right, and I can’t figure out how.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel an overwhelming need to confess your thoughts or actions to someone, like a priest or a family member, to feel relieved?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. I don’t know if it makes me relieved exactly, but I keep trying. I keep telling people, because I feel like they need to know what I did, or what I failed to do.\n\nI’ve told my family, and I’ve said it at church too, that I’m the reason my friend died. And it’s not just that. I didn’t call her back when I should have, and I keep thinking if I had just picked up the phone, if I had been less selfish, less full of those ugly thoughts I had sometimes… maybe she’d still be here. And then it feels like it didn’t stop with her. It feels like the Lord saw all of it, every part of my heart, and now everybody at church is being punished because of me.\n\nSo I don’t know what I’m supposed to do to stop it. I pray, I tell people, I try to stay away sometimes because I don’t want to bring that punishment near them. But I don’t know if that helps. I just feel like if I don’t do something, if I don’t keep confessing it or trying to make them see, then something else terrible could happen because of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do something to undo the harm you think you've caused?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, I do feel that. I don’t know how to undo it, that’s the terrible part. And I had ugly thoughts sometimes too… hard, selfish thoughts, things I’m ashamed of. I keep thinking the Lord saw all of it, not just what I did or didn’t do, but what was in me.\n\nSo I feel like the harm didn’t just stop with her. I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, because of what I failed to do and because of what was in my heart. And I keep trying to confess it, to explain it, like maybe if I say it plainly enough or admit it enough, somehow it will undo a little of what I caused. But it doesn’t. It never really does. It just sits there.\n\nDoctor: When you confess your thoughts and actions to others, are you hoping to avoid punishment or to feel forgiven?\n\nPatient: I think… both, maybe. I don’t know how to separate them anymore.\n\nWhen I tell people, when I say it out loud again, that I’m the reason my friend died, part of me is hoping somebody will say something that makes it not true. Or that the Lord will hear me admitting it and maybe… maybe He’ll stop being angry. Because I didn’t call her back. I keep coming back to that. She reached out, and I failed her. And it wasn’t just the phone call, it was what was in me too, those ugly thoughts I had sometimes, the selfishness, the hardness in my heart. I feel like He saw that.\n\nSo yes, I guess I’m hoping to be forgiven, but I don’t really feel like I deserve it. And I’m scared of the punishment too, not just for me. I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, like my sin spilled over onto them somehow. So when I confess, I’m trying to make it known, trying to say, “It was me, I did this, don’t punish them.” But it doesn’t feel like enough.\n\nPeople tell me I’m not responsible, but they don’t understand. I’m not confessing just to feel better. I’m confessing because it feels true, and because if I don’t say it, then it’s like I’m hiding it from God and from everyone. And I’m afraid that would make it worse.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts come into your mind without you wanting them to?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I don’t know if I’d say they come in without me wanting them to, exactly. That’s part of what scares me, because I keep wondering what it says about me that they were there at all.\n\nSometimes it feels like I’m just trying to pray or sit quietly, and then I remember something awful I thought or felt, or I worry that maybe I meant it more than I want to admit. But I don’t know if that’s the same as them “coming into my mind.” It’s more like I keep turning them over and over, trying to figure out if they were really mine, if they were sinful, if they caused something.\n\nI don’t want to be the kind of person who has thoughts like that. I can say that. I don’t want them. But then I think… if they came from me, then maybe wanting them gone now doesn’t excuse that they were there. I’m sorry, I don’t know if I’m answering the way you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are a part of who you are, or do they feel alien to you?\n\nPatient: They feel like… I don’t know. I wish I could say they were alien, like they were just some strange thing passing through me that didn’t belong to me. That would be a comfort, I suppose. But I don’t feel that way, not really.\n\nI keep thinking they must be part of me, because they came from inside me. Those ugly thoughts, the selfish ones, the hard ones… even if I didn’t want to think of myself that way, they were there. Like the thoughts showed what was really in my heart, and then I failed her when it mattered.\n\nPeople try to tell me, “That isn’t who you are,” but I don’t know how they can know that. The Lord knows. He saw it. He saw what I thought, and He saw what I didn’t do. And I feel like everybody at church is being punished because of me, because of what was in me and because I didn’t answer her. So no… I don’t think they feel alien. They feel like evidence. Like proof of what I really am, even if I hate saying that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are forced upon you and are not something you would normally think?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if “forced upon me” is the right way to say it. That sounds like they’re coming from somewhere else, or like I’m being made to think them, and I don’t… I don’t really know that I can say that.\n\nThey don’t feel normal, exactly. I don’t like them. I don’t sit there wanting to have ugly thoughts or wanting to feel hard-hearted. But when they come up, or when I remember them, it feels like they’re mine somehow. Like they came out of me, and that’s what makes it so upsetting. If they were just forced on me, maybe I could push them away and say, “That’s not me.” But I can’t seem to do that.\n\nI keep going back and forth in my head, trying to decide what it means that I had them at all. Maybe other people have bad thoughts and just let them pass, I don’t know. For me it feels like I have to examine them, like I have to know whether they show something about me. So no, I’m not sure I’d say they’re forced upon me. I’d say they feel wrong, and I wish they weren’t there, but I’m afraid they are something I would think, and that’s what frightens me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are in line with your own values and beliefs?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, yes and no. They’re not in line with what I was raised to believe, or what I want to believe. I don’t want to be ugly-hearted. I don’t want to be selfish or cold. I’ve tried to be a good Christian woman, I’ve tried to do right by people. So in that way, no, those thoughts don’t feel like what my values are supposed to be.\n\nBut then I keep thinking… if they were in me, if I had them, then maybe that means my values weren’t real enough. Maybe the Lord saw what was really underneath. That’s what I can’t get away from. I keep telling people I’m the reason my friend died, because she reached out and I didn’t call her back, and because I had ugly thoughts sometimes, and I feel like those things are tied together. Like it wasn’t just a missed phone call, it was proof that something was wrong in me.\n\nAnd now I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. I know it sounds terrible to say out loud, but I can’t see it any other way. The Lord knows what was in my heart, even if I tried to act decent on the outside. So no, they don’t match what I want my beliefs to be… but I’m afraid they show what I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something you would normally think and agree with, or are they disturbing to you and go against what you believe in?\n\nPatient: They’re disturbing to me, Doctor. I don’t want to agree with them. I don’t want to be the kind of person who has ugly thoughts in her heart. But the trouble is, I keep thinking that because I had them at all, they must mean something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it before, but I keep telling them—I keep telling people—I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out, and I didn’t call her back. And then I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, and it all feels tied together, like it shows I was selfish, or cold, or not the kind of Christian woman I thought I was.\n\nSo no, they don’t go along with what I believe in, not what I want to believe in. But I can’t just say, “Oh, those weren’t me,” either. The Lord saw all of it. He saw I didn’t answer her, and He saw what was in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. That’s what I can’t get away from. It feels like judgment. Like proof.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these thoughts are something that you would normally think and are in line with your own personality?\n\nPatient: No… not in line with who I thought I was. I keep saying that because I don’t know how else to explain it. I didn’t think of myself as someone who would be cruel or uncaring. I tried to be decent. I tried to be faithful. But then I look at what happened, and I think, well, maybe this is who I really was underneath.\n\nI keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back. So when I put it all together, I can’t just say, “That wasn’t me.” It feels like it came from me, and that’s what scares me.\n\nAnd I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. Like my sin, my coldness, whatever was in my heart, has brought trouble down on them. So no, it’s not the personality I wanted to have, but I’m afraid it shows the truth about me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally think and are a part of who you are?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want them to be part of who I am. That’s the honest answer. I don’t want to think that way, and I don’t feel proud of it, not at all. But I keep coming back to the same thing, Doctor—if those ugly thoughts were in me, even for a moment, then maybe they do show something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it already, and I’m sorry, but I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me and I didn’t call her back. I keep seeing that over and over in my mind, like there was this chance to do the right thing and I failed her. And then when I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, it feels like it all fits together, like maybe I was cold or selfish and didn’t even see it clearly at the time.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t have said those thoughts were normally me. I would’ve said I was trying to be a decent woman. But now… now I can’t separate them from me. I feel like the Lord saw what was really in my heart. That’s the honest answer. I don’t want to think that way, and I don’t feel proud of it, not at all. But I keep coming back to the same thing, Doctor—if those ugly thoughts were in me, even for a moment, then maybe they do show something about me.\n\nI know I’ve said it already, and I’m sorry, but I keep telling them I’m the reason my friend died. She reached out to me and I didn’t call her back. I keep seeing that over and over in my mind, like there was this chance to do the right thing and I failed her. And then when I think about the ugly thoughts I had sometimes, it feels like it all fits together, like maybe I was cold or selfish and didn’t even see it clearly at the time.\n\nSo no, I wouldn’t have said those thoughts were normally me. I would’ve said I was trying to be a decent woman. But now… now I can’t separate them from me. I feel like the Lord saw what was really in my heart. And I know everybody at church is being punished because of me. I know that sounds awful, but it feels true. It feels like my fault has spread onto them somehow. So I don’t want it to be who I am, but I’m afraid it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are something that you would normally think and are a part of who you are, or do they feel like they are coming from outside of yourself?\n\nPatient: They don’t feel like a voice from outside, if that’s what you mean. They feel like they’re in me, and that’s the worst part. I wish I could say they were just something strange that came from somewhere else, something I could push away and say, “That isn’t mine.” But I can’t do that.\n\nI keep going back to my friend. She reached out, and I didn’t call her back. I keep telling people that I’m the reason she died, because I didn’t do what I should have done. And then when I remember that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I’m ashamed of, it feels like it all came from the same place inside me. Like it shows there was something wrong with my heart.\n\nI know people keep telling me not to say that, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. That doesn’t feel like it’s coming from outside myself either. It feels like something I know, like a truth I can’t get away from. So… no, not outside. It feels like it’s me. And I hate that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like these ugly thoughts are consistent with your own identity and values?\n\nPatient: No… I mean, they’re not consistent with what I wanted my values to be. I always thought I tried to be a decent person, tried to do right by people, tried to be faithful. But now I keep thinking maybe that was just what I wanted to believe about myself.\n\nBecause if I really had good values, why didn’t I call my friend back? She reached out to me, and I didn’t call her back, and I keep telling them I’m the reason she died. I can’t get away from that. It feels like proof that there was something selfish or cold in me. And then I remember that I had ugly thoughts sometimes too, thoughts I shouldn’t have had, thoughts I’m ashamed were even in my head, and it makes me feel like maybe those thoughts were closer to the truth of me than all the good things I tried to be.\n\nAnd I know people say I’m taking too much onto myself, but I know the Lord is punishing everybody at church because of me. That’s what makes it feel like it is about who I am, not just a passing thought. It feels like something wrong in me has caused harm beyond just me. So… no, they don’t match the person I wanted to be. But I’m afraid they show who I really am."
